Its noise is built from the difference of two real projections taken at the same angle (inside the object the model underestimates the measured noise by a factor 1.25); basis 0 carries 5.06 times the single-energy noise (condition number 15.5). The flat fields are 3.5 times quieter than one projection at the same binning, so noise built from them would be underestimated. [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_real_walnut_cbct.py)
  Terms: cone-beam CT — A CT geometry in which a point source illuminates a flat-panel detector with a cone of X-rays, so one rotation records a whole volume.; FDK — An approximate cone-beam reconstruction that weights the projections, ramp-filters them row by row and back-projects them; it is exact only in the mid-plane of the orbit.; SIRT — An iterative reconstruction that back-projects the residual of all projections at once and corrects the image step by step, usable where no inversion formula exists.; OS-SART — An iterative method that splits the projections into ordered subsets and applies the SIRT update one subset at a time, which converges faster early on.; total variation — The sum of gradient magnitudes of an image; regularising with it favours piecewise-constant images and fills missing data with flat patches.
- [Carte de choix du matériel : les mêmes défauts vus par 60 combinaisons éclairage, caméra, objectif](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_equipment_selection_map/02_equipment_at_a_glance.png) (figure): We pre-tested visual inspection equipment on the same flaw. With 60 combinations of lighting, camera and lens we mapped how small a flaw is found, with the spread across repeats. It is a synthetic test, not the performance of real equipment.
  Inspection equipment is usually chosen one component at a time, each shown at its best by its own vendor. This synthetic test images the same parts and flaws with six lights (coaxial, dome, 70-degree ring, 30-degree ring, 10-degree dark field, and a phase-shifted fringe screen for deflectometry) x five cameras (monochrome, colour, polarisation, SWIR, hyperspectral) x two lenses (telecentric, ordinary), and reports a90/95 (MIL-HDBK-1823A). Every cell sets its threshold for a 1 % false-call rate on the flaw-free sites it is scored on (a definition for comparing at equal false-call rate; a threshold carried over from training gives 1.1 % pooled and up to 8 % in a single cell). Exposure is capped at 10 ms per field of view (photon counts are not equalised), the coaxial diffuser is twice the field width, and three site seed sets give median and min-max. Machined aluminium scratch parallel to the polishing lines: coaxial light with a telecentric lens is among the best (monochrome 39 um, colour 34 um); dark field with monochrome finds it under every seed set at 76-79 um, and a 30-degree ring with monochrome at 73-74 um at the full budget (at the small CI budget it flips to x with the seeds). Whether bright field needs a telecentric lens depends on the coaxial size: with a diffuser as wide as the field, an ordinary lens detects the largest scratches 80 % of the time (telecentric 100 %); at twice the field an ordinary lens also finds them (92 um, 2.4 times the telecentric value). Dome and high ring confuse the scratch with the polishing lines; the hyperspectral camera's 128 um pixel is too coarse. Shallow paint dent: coaxial, dome and 30-degree ring find nothing with any camera. The fringe screen finds it in all 10 cells (best 21 um) and raking dark field also in all 10 (best 28 um, 1.3 times the fringe value with a cheaper light). The floor is orange peel: cutting its slope from 0.10 to 0.02 degrees takes the fringe a90/95 from 22 to 4.8 um. Raking light wobbles at the small CI budget but settles at the full budget. The dome shows a dent as a bright blob only where its camera hole is mirrored in the slope (60-80 mm from the field centre), so detection moves between 0.33 and 0.92 across the field. The line-scan hyperspectral answer depends on the exposure cap: for the raking-light dent it is 214 um at 10 ms per field (a 30 kHz line rate no real instrument reaches), 16 um at 100 ms, 9.6 um at 1 s, and 9.9 um with the bands simply summed, so it wins on electrons. Black burn speck in resin: found by every set except the fringe screen; a 30-degree ring with a colour camera and ordinary lens (89 um) is enough. White PE fragment in white PP: no visible camera finds it, which is the input (the material table gives both the same visible absorption), not a finding; one SWIR band sees -0.22 % and fails; the 10 nm x 71 band camera finds it under the dome at 678-746 um (about 2 mm under coaxial light). 67 cells hit the exposure cap. Real data (both CC BY 4.0) support only the camera-band axis for polymers, as pixel-level AUC: plastic pieces on pork belly (Ghimpeteanu et al., Zenodo doi:10.5281/zenodo.17242553) go from 0.493 (chance) with one SWIR band to 0.937 / 0.959 / 0.994 with 2 / 6 / 184 bands; PP vs PE fragments (Arbash et al., RODARE doi:10.14278/rodare.3390) reach about 0.63 with monochrome, colour or one SWIR band and 0.882 with 950-1650 nm spectra in 20 nm bins (0.754 when extended to 2231 nm with 110 training pixels). The binning, the window and the real-data gate thresholds were chosen after seeing the data. Lighting, lens and the um values are comparisons inside this synthetic test, not hardware specs; many material values, light levels and price tiers are estimates. a90/95 is the Wald (delta-method) upper bound, and each fit uses 90 flawed sites (MIL-HDBK-1823A recommends at least 40 for a-hat data; the reduced CI run uses 36). The Wald bound is optimistic: in 2000 trials on synthetic data with a known a90, it is at or above the true a90 in 0.89 of fits with 40 sites and 0.92 with 100 (nominal 0.95). A likelihood-ratio bound would be about 4 % larger at the median (39 % or more in the top quarter). The values above are the Wald ones. [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_equipment_selection_map.py)
  Terms: SWIR — The short-wave infrared band, roughly around 1 to 3 µm, where material absorption can distinguish objects that look alike in visible light.; a90/95 — The flaw size at which the lower one-sided 95% confidence bound on the POD reaches 90%.
- [Bruit égal, mais les défauts faibles se cachent sur fond réel](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_real_defect_floor/03_defect_floor_explained.mp4) (vidéo): Inspection teams often estimate how faint a scratch they can catch on a synthetic image of a flat field plus noise. With the noise level matched, a real brick background raised the detection limit by up to 3.47 times. The limit was set not by noise but by structure in the background, such as mortar joints.
  Le même défaut, de plus en plus marqué, est inséré dans un fond réel (briques) et dans un fond synthétique de niveau de bruit identique. **Même à niveau de bruit égal, la limite de détection sur le fond réel est de 2.03 à 3.47 fois celle du fond synthétique (pour des défauts de position et d'amplitude connues).** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_real_defect_floor.py)
- [Trouver les vides du plan de collage par la phase de l'écho ultrasonore](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_sam_hybrid_bonding_voids/03_amplitude_vs_phase.png) (figure): Small voids in the bonding plane of stacked chips are searched for with a scanning acoustic microscope. Every void of 16 um or more appeared inverted and bright in the echo-phase image. In the brightness image many small voids hide among good pixels.
  A thinned 100 um silicon die over a hybrid bond (SiO2 1 + 1 um, Cu pads 1 + 1 um of 40 um diameter at 80 um pitch) with voids of 5 to 24 um placed on pads and in the dielectric, imaged by a focused scanning acoustic microscope (1 mm of water, F/3); echoes come from a layer transfer matrix checked against a bottom-up Airy recursion (Airy multiple-reflection formula), a time-domain ray sum and the optical thin-film formula to 1e-12. Read at the expected time of the bond plane, the dielectric echo is at -91 deg and the Cu pad at +84 deg (thin layers give quadrature echoes) while voids are inverted (-175 deg in the dielectric, +159 deg on a pad). In the amplitude image 8 of 12 voids hide inside the brightness range of intact pixels: an inverted echo adds at right angles to a quadrature background, so voids of 9 to 16 um change the amplitude by 0.4 to 3.5 sigma but the phase-aware statistic by 3.0 to 8.9 sigma; the phase image flags every void of 16 um and up and 1 of 26,724 intact pixels. The conventional single-sample polarity of an intact Cu pad flips 5 times between 140 and 400 MHz. The optimal frequency has a closed form (139.16 MHz for a 10 um void, equal to brute force) and the front-echo tail sets a floor of 130 MHz.  **POD (a-hat vs a, 88 voids): a90/95 of the phase-aware detector is 12.4 / 13.9 / 57.6 um at 150 / 200 / 300 MHz against 16.6 / 19.3 / 101.6 um for amplitude only, under the same baseline and threshold rule. Synthetic: 1-D plane-wave longitudinal layers, a Gaussian lateral PSF, no refraction aberration, silicon attenuation set to 0 (unverified), no kissing bonds.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_sam_hybrid_bonding_voids.py)
  Terms: SAM — An instrument that scans focused ultrasound over a specimen and images internal interfaces or defects from echo amplitude, phase, or arrival time.; Airy — The formula for a parallel plate's transmittance obtained by summing its infinite series of multiple reflections.
- [Cartons mélangés : marche de profondeur, rainure en V ou couleur](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_mixed_case_depalletizing/08_depal_robot_video.mp4) (vidéo): Packed cartons with identical prints are told apart from overhead depth and colour, then unloaded. A depth step splits none, and whether depth dips or dark colour lines win flips across 28 flap-seam builds. With identical prints, deep grooves favour colour; only wide, untaped cuts over shallow grooves favour depth.
  Cartons of different sizes, prints, tape, labels, crush and shrink wrap are stacked two layers high on a JIS T11 pallet and imaged by an overhead RGB-D camera (2.70 m, 960 x 720), 10 pallets for each of 5 conditions (self-made scenes, not external real data). In the base scenes each carton has a 4 mm edge radius and a flap-seam groove along the middle of its top (0.8-2.0 mm, no gap, kraft tape on 70 % and clear tape on 30 %); 40 % of the tops bulge by 3-8 mm. Colour is rendered with Blender Cycles; depth comes from the same geometry through the physics of a pattern-projection sensor. Top faces are segmented with depth_to_organized_points, bilateral_filter_depth, fill_holes, normals_from_depth and watersheds_marker and measured with ransac_plane and fit_rectangle2. Five cues, all classical methods by the same author: depth step / depth V-groove (a step region split along a shallow depth groove, read with crop_rectangle2) / colour only (a weak baseline) / colour groove (a step region split along a dark colour groove) / both (AND). Base-scene first-frame recall (IoU >= 0.5, median of 10 seeds), step / V-groove / colour only / colour groove / both: tight 0.39 / 0.63 / 0.29 / 0.64 / 0.45, identical 0.00 / 0.23 / 0.17 / 0.69 / 0.67, gaps 1.00 / 0.88 / 0.44 / 1.00 / 1.00, shrink 0.44 / 0.32 / 0.35 / 0.50 / 0.48, crushed 0.56 / 0.60 / 0.48 / 0.74 / 0.62. On the unseen seeds 3-9 alone: tight 0.33 / 0.60 / 0.29 / 0.57 / 0.40 (the V-groove slightly ahead), identical 0.00 / 0.17 / 0.17 / 0.71 / 0.67. The V-groove leaves a median 2.5-9.0 false detections per image, the colour groove none. With tight seams the V-groove and the colour groove find about as many faces and the colour groove over-splits less; with identical neighbours the V-groove also splits at the flap seam. Flap-seam map (tight and identical, median of seeds 3-5, all cartons built alike, 28 builds): groove depth 0.2 / 0.5 / 1.0 / 2.0 mm x gap 0 / 0.5 / 1.5 mm x tape (kraft / clear / none). On identical neighbours, under kraft or no tape, the V-groove matches or beats the colour groove when the groove is 1 mm or less (kraft 0.5 mm: 1.00 against 0.83) and collapses at 2 mm, where the colour groove wins (kraft 2 mm: 0.29 against 0.83). With an untaped 1.5 mm gap over a groove of 1 mm or less the colour groove also splits along the dark line of the seam and the V-groove is ahead (1 mm groove: 1.00 against 0.71; 0.2 and 0.5 mm: 1.00 against 0.83; with a 0.5 mm gap they tie). Under clear tape the V-groove drops to 0.57-0.67 even without a gap and the winner varies by cell (0.5 mm gap, 2 mm groove: both 0.29-0.43). With tight seams the differences are small, flipping within 0.57-0.82. The base scenes sat on the deep, gap-free side, which favoured the colour groove. Dependence on the scene: shrinking the edge radius from 4 to 2 to 1 mm takes V-groove recall on identical neighbours from 0.29 to 0.00 to 0.00, and without flap seams and bulges it is 0.83 (numerically rendered, 5 seeds). VALLEY_MIN = 0.5 mm was set after looking at seeds 0-2. The claim gates were set after seeing the results (base scenes on all and on unseen seeds, the map on 3 seeds). The gap-condition floor (>= 0.85) was already there for the step and AND; the colour groove was added after seeing the results, and with the CI stand-in colour (seed 0) it sits at 0.86, a margin of 0.01. Pick-one-and-re-image success (10 seeds, rule-based judgement): tight 0.36 / 0.44 / 0.31 / 0.58 / 0.53, identical 0.12 / 0.28 / 0.23 / 0.53 / 0.44, crushed 0.32 / 0.28 / 0.26 / 0.48 / 0.42, gaps 0.91 / 0.61 / 0.33 / 0.90 / 0.89 (footprint across two cartons = double pick; off the edge, plane residual over 6 mm or peel-moment ratio over 1 = drop; carton on top = snag; carton plus 1.8 kg of tooling above the 12.5 kg payload = overload, which the 11 kg type D exceeds at 12.8 kg). Real-data chapter: on HOPE (real depth from an active-stereo depth camera, 50 val images, 325 faces of box-shaped objects; tabletop toy grocery boxes seen obliquely at 0.5-1 m) the plane-then-rectangle measurement with the same set of operators (segmentation steps and thresholds implemented separately) gives recall 0.705, long-side error median -2.0 mm (absolute median 5.6 mm), normal error 2.6 degrees and 43 false detections per image (background 1395, non-box objects 622, other faces of scored boxes 118, heavily occluded boxes 19). It does not support the headline claim. Credit: Tyree, Tremblay, To, Cheng, Mosier, Smith, Birchfield, IROS 2022 (BOP format). The bundled dataset_info.md says 'Licensed under the CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 license' while the BOP download page says 'license: CC BY-SA 4.0'; we treat it as non-commercial and show numbers only. The video (about 48 s) shows, per condition, one successful and one failed pick by the colour-groove cue with a UR10e (MuJoCo Menagerie model, BSD-3-Clause, plain colours; not affiliated with, endorsed or warranted by any company) on a linear axis driven by inverse kinematics. Limits: the scenes and thresholds come from the same author (no external basis for the edge radius or the flap-seam build; clear tape is assumed transparent to both depth and colour); with the stand-in colour used in CI the ranking of the colour-based cues flips, so the CI gates cover only depth-only claims and the gap floor. [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_mixed_case_depalletizing.py)
- [Appariement par codes résistant aux reflets et à l'occultation](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_code_matching_breakdown/05_highlight_sweep.gif) (GIF): Normalised cross-correlation, the usual template matcher, fails when part of the target is hidden or glare washes it out. Matchers comparing the sign of brightness changes were tested on 40 templates from public real photos. They withstand occlusion far better, yet every method stays weak to rotation.
  Normalised cross-correlation (NCC) compares brightness values, so it is fragile when part of the template is occluded, saturated by a highlight or darkened on one side. Four matchers that compare the direction of brightness change as codes (orientation code matching, increment sign correlation, the selective correlation coefficient and the Radial Reach Filter, re-implemented from the published equations) are added as six codematch ops and scored on 40 templates of 32 x 32 cut from five public CC0 photographs. Half the hits are lost at 10 % occlusion for NCC, 36 % for SCC, 63 % for ISC and 67 % for OCM; at 6 %, 10 %, 44 % and 53 % saturated highlight. All four are weak to rotation (7-9 degrees). SCC selects only pixels whose codes agree, which inflates correlation at unrelated positions (selection bias); taken literally it broke before NCC, and a floor at the uncorrelated usage rate plus 4 sigma fixes it.  **On 11 flat sky templates NCC misses 10 with correlation around 0.82, while the code matchers refuse or answer correctly (no wrong answer). Gates: a brute-force second implementation, the existing NCC, invariance to monotone maps, commutation with rot90 (broken by a probe that swaps the cyclic difference for the absolute one) and a closed-form model of the values at the true position (|z| <= 3.6 over 1,080 trials).** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_code_matching_breakdown.py)
  Terms: OCM — A template-matching method that quantizes each pixel's gradient orientation and compares the resulting orientation codes.
- [Résolution 20x dans un champ 4x (ptychographie de Fourier)](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_fourier_ptychography/01_usaf_4x_fp_20x.png) (figure): A low-magnification objective sees a wide field but misses fine detail. Combining images lit from different angles in frequency space resolved detail as fine as a 20x image over the same wide field. When the illumination overlap is too small to converge, no answer is returned.
  Keep the wide field of a low-magnification objective, take a set of images under LED illumination from many angles, stitch them in frequency space and recover a 20x-equivalent resolution and the phase by iterative phase retrieval. Six new ops (`fourierptycho`, category `fpm` of `opsoptics`): closed-form design numbers before measuring (synthetic NA, cut-off period, pupil overlap, LED count, sampling, LEDs on the bright-/dark-field boundary), a USAF target, a capture simulator (noise, aberrations, LED misalignment, reflective heights), an entry point for measured data, a reconstruction with joint pupil recovery and LED position correction that returns its convergence history, stop reason and synthetic NA and refuses unless it converges, and an element-by-element USAF read-out. With a 4x / NA 0.1 objective and 13 x 13 LEDs (synthetic NA 0.424) one image resolves G7 E4 (5.53 um; closed form 5.32 um) and FP resolves G9 E5 (1.22 um; closed form 1.25 um), the same element as a 20x / NA 0.40 coherent image. Phase within 0.13 rad rms of the band-limited truth; reflective 20-40 nm steps within 4.0 nm rms. Lowering the pupil overlap from 64 % to 7 %, 43 % and above converge while 32 % and below are refused, bracketing the published 35 % minimum; the residual alone cannot tell (18 % overlap has a smaller residual than 64 %). Pupil aberrations of 0.12 waves rms are recovered to 0.005 waves and an LED array offset of (0.6, -0.4) mm, 1 deg and 1.5 mm is read back as (0.62, -0.36) mm, 0.93 deg and 1.25 mm. Flipping the sign of k yields the phase-conjugate image with the same residual.  **On public FP data of a USAF target (not shipped; run only when present) FP resolves G9 E3 (1.55 um) against the closed-form 1.60 um from the published set-up; a single raw image resolves G7 E3. Nothing learned, nothing calibrated, 33 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_fourier_ptychography.py)
- [Le cône de lumière décide si creux et rayures sont visibles](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_illumination_cone_vs_na/02_works_by_cone.png) (figure): On near-mirror metal parts, shallow dents and scratches on a hairline finish show or vanish with the width of the cone of light more than with the lamp type. With a cone half-angle of 4°, the hairline streaks disappear and only the scratch stays dark. A single dent can appear or disappear as the cone opens.
  For shallow dents, scratches on a hairline finish and satin unevenness on near-specular metal parts, what shows is set less by the type of light than by the half-angle α and axis of the cone of light reaching each point, relative to the cone the camera accepts (object-side NA, half-angle β): the illumination-NA / objective-NA relation of Koehler illumination and the knife edge of schlieren optics. Reflection is an isometry of the sphere of directions, so the brightness is the intersection of two spherical caps, a closed form: slopes below |α − β|/2 do not show, slopes beyond (α + β)/2 go dark. Five new ops in `illumdesign` (`solid_angle_light`, `solid_angle_boundaries`, `solid_angle_brightness`, `solid_angle_sweep`, `solid_angle_render`). The closed form agrees with the projected-solid-angle pupil integral to 0.002. Through a telecentric lens of NA 0.033 a 2 µm dent (steepest slope 0.25°) shows as a ring only with α = β (contrast −0.091 measured on the image, closed form to 1e-4) and vanishes at α = 0.5° and 7°, as the closed form predicts. The closed form picks α between 3.5° and 6.1° to hide hairline marks (99th-percentile slope 0.82°) while blackening a 4° scratch: at α = 4° the marks fluctuate 0.014 times as much as at α = β and the scratch contrast is −1.00. Tilting the cone by β puts the flat ground at 0.39 and splits the dent's flanks into 0.53 and 0.25 (closed form 0.54 / 0.25), so the slope sign reads as bright or dark. Satin patches (micro-facet spread 1.3° against 0.8°) give −0.20 at α = β and −0.02 at α = 7°.  **Where it breaks: ±0.25° waviness makes the background swing 1.6 times the dent's dip at α = β, so a threshold cannot pick the dent; a micro-facet spread of 0.25° already cuts the contrast to a third, and at 4° no cone gets above 0.001. No comparison photographs taken with only the illumination solid angle changed were found under a reusable licence, so the check against real images is still open. Nothing learned, nothing calibrated, 15 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_illumination_cone_vs_na.py)
- [Étalonnage sur une image télécentrique réelle, écarts montrés](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_simcal_telecentric/02_real_model_residuals.png) (figure): An imaging model was fitted to a published real image of a tilted alignment plate seen through a telecentric lens. The magnification came out at 252.14 px/mm, close to the paper's value. Places where the model does not fit are kept and drawn as residual needles on the image.
  An imaging model with plate tilt, orientation, scale, radial distortion k1 and the inverse entrance-pupil distance 1/L (telecentricity) is calibrated with simcal on the ring centrelines of a real 29.5-degree tilted alignment plate (Stark et al., Sci. Rep. 2025, CC BY 4.0). With a noise level independent of the model (local-quadratic pure error, 1.12 px) the absolute chi-square gate fails (p = 7e-9) and the numbers are withdrawn: the 1.35 px residual carries smooth ring-by-ring discrepancy. Adding it as white variance passes all six gates, and only after checking that a ring jackknife agrees with the Laplace uncertainty are numbers reported: 252.14 ± 0.18 px/mm (paper K1 = 252.0) and 1/L = 0.50 ± 0.18 per metre, i.e. the entrance pupil is farther than 1.16 m (2 sigma), which rules out the ordinary f = 100 mm (302 mm) and f = 25 mm lenses.  **On synthetic data the eight coefficients are recovered at the bound, the ordinary lens's 1/L = 3.31 per metre within 0.1 sigma of the closed form, an untilted plate makes the orientation 'no influence', and a low-order discrepancy makes Laplace 2.4 times optimistic, which the jackknife catches. The 2.7-sigma offset of 1/L from zero is unresolved. No training.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_simcal_telecentric.py)
- [Plans d'installation et de lentilles issus directement du calcul optique](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_optics_drawings/06_setup_ring_coaxial.png) (figure): In meetings about inspection systems, the lens layout and the setup drawing are the common language. The optical calculation now draws them directly, up to ISO 10110 element drawings. Each written file is read back, and its lines and text are scored against the calculation.
  In inspection and metrology meetings the lens layout and the setup drawing (working distance, field of view, lighting angles) are the common language, and ordering a lens needs an ISO 10110 element drawing. Fullseye evaluated prescriptions with real rays and produced optical budgets, but drew nothing. Eight new ops (`optdraw`, category `drawing` of `opsoptics`) draw a meridional lens layout (surface sags, edges, stop, image plane, real rays aimed at the stop and coloured by field, EFL / BFL / track / image-height dimensions and a prescription table), a side and top view of a vision setup (field-of-view cone, working distance, depth-of-field band, five light families with elevation angles) and an ISO 10110 three-field element drawing whose tolerances come from the `tolerance_analysis` dictionary, and write them as SVG (true scale at 100 %) and DXF R12 with layers and line types using only the standard library. The gates score the files read back, not the picture: two textbook Cooke triplets match their published EFL to 7e-6 and 2e-5, drawn surfaces match the closed-form sphere sag to 6e-7 mm, ray kinks lie on the surfaces to 5e-7 mm, chief rays match `raytrace.chief_ray` to 5e-7 mm and dimension texts match at the printed precision. Telecentric lenses with the entrance pupil at infinity draw too, their chief-ray angles agreeing with `telecentric.chief_ray_angle` (a separate implementation) to 3e-7 degrees. An open-source DXF library (MIT) read all eleven files with zero audit errors.  **Inspecting the setup drawing at full size exposed a real bug: the drawn field of view (sensor × WD / f) and the budget magnification f/(WD − f) disagreed by 14 % in the same drawing, because `optscene.vision_layout` placed the pinhole at the focal length instead of the image distance f·WD/(WD − f); it is fixed, and a gate now counts the pixels of a 12 mm plate in a captured image (124 px against 124.2). Broken drawings turn every gate red. No training, no calibration, 53 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_optics_drawings.py)
- [Croissance de fissure : compter les pixels surestime la vitesse de 2.5 fois](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_crack_width_timeseries/11_series_video.gif) (GIF): The same wall is photographed again over the years to measure how fast a crack widens. Thresholding and counting pixels overstated the growth rate by +153.0 %. Integrating the loss of brightness across the crack gets the rate nearly right.
  A wall at 1 px = 0.15 mm, rephotographed for twelve epochs over three years, with a crack opening at a true 0.040 mm/yr. Thresholding and counting pixels understates the width by 25.0 % yet overstates the growth rate by +153.0 %, and invents +0.0117 mm/yr of growth in a control where the width is frozen (the culprit is blur, isolated by switching one nuisance at a time).  **Integrating the intensity deficit gives +3.1 % on the rate and +0.0005 mm/yr on the control. The thresholded rate swings from 0.0241 to 0.1038 mm/yr on initial width alone — it depends on whether a one-pixel step happens to fall inside the observation window.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_crack_width_timeseries.py)
- [Deux réseaux invisibles : les franges de moiré lisent des décalages nanométriques](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_moire_alignment/01_marks.png) (figure): Lithography must overlay a mask and a wafer to within nanometres. Overlaying two gratings too fine for the camera to see produces 102 µm fringes that magnify the shift. Reading the fringe position recovers shifts smaller than a nanometre.
  Alignment in lithography and nanoimprint overlays a mask and a wafer to nanometres. Two gratings of slightly different pitch (mask 2.00 µm, wafer 2.04 µm) are each erased by the camera blur (the direct image has a standard deviation of 9e-6), yet overlaid they make 102 µm fringes that magnify the shift fiftyfold. Eight new ops (`moirealign`, category `moire_align` of `opsoptics`): magnification and capture range, the Talbot effect of the proximity gap, the synthetic mark image, the fringe phase by lock-in (least squares at the known frequency) or by Takeda's FFT method, phase to shift, the differential pair, reading a single, differential or four-quadrant 2-D mark, and the noise lower bound. The magnification depends on which grating moves: measured from the images 50.000 for the wafer and 51.000 for the mask (the often quoted M = p_w/|Δp| is the mask-moving case). Eleven 1 nm steps are read with an RMS error of 0.08 nm, and the Monte-Carlo spread matches the phase lower bound (Rife-Boorstyn times the Hann factor 1.5 times 2 for a noisy reference) with a ratio of 1.00. Against the baseline (a resolvable 20 µm bar mark read by cross-correlation and parabolic sub-pixel interpolation) the RMS error is 91 times smaller, but most of that is the parabola's bias (it reads 17 % short); the random error alone differs only 2.5-fold (0.077 vs 0.19 nm), and the breakdown is printed. With a coarser camera (0.5 to 2 µm pixels) the correlation worsens 2.3-fold while the moiré does not change. Two pairs with opposite pitch differences cancel a 300 nm drift of the whole mark (one pair alone is off by 300/M = 6.2 nm) and a 30 % illumination tilt to within 0.03 nm, and a 2 % camera-scale error moves the reading by 0.007 nm because the conversion depends only on the pitches (the correlation is off by the full 2 %). The reading wraps every pitch (1.01 µm for the differential pair); a coarse correlation fixes the order and reads 0 to 5 µm with 0.14 nm RMS. Across a proximity gap the fringes return at the Talbot length (the lensless self-image distance 2p²/λ) of 14.5 µm and flip sign at half of it, and a flip between reference and measurement makes the reading jump by half a period (1020 nm).  **Honest limit: the 0.1 nm figures are a white-noise floor; published alignment accuracies of 10 to 30 nm are set by systematic errors such as mark asymmetry, gap and tilt. Nothing learned, nothing calibrated, 32 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_moire_alignment.py)
  Terms: Moiré — A long-period fringe produced at the difference spatial frequency when two patterns with slightly different pitch or orientation are superposed.
- [Le speckle laser fixe le plancher de précision en hauteur de la triangulation laser](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_laser_line_speckle_floor/01_line.png) (figure): When a laser line measures height, speckle, the grainy pattern of interfering light, breaks up the line. Reading position by the centroid still leaves an error of 6.4 µm, close to the floor the formula predicts. Averaging frames of a static part does not help; only moving the part lowers it.
  A sequel to the weld-bead scan: the same laser line, re-rendered by coherent imaging (rough-surface phase, observation pupil, |E|^2) instead of a smooth band plus additive noise; the truth is the height put into the rendering. The three-point log-parabola that beat the brightest-pixel estimator 8.6-fold under additive noise falls to the brightest pixel's error under speckle (12.7 vs 13.1 µm) and is twice as bad as the centroid (6.4 µm, 0.93 of Dorsch's floor C·λ/(2π sin u sin θ) = 6.9 µm) - three samples around the peak follow the brightest grain.  **Averaging 100 frames of a static part leaves the error at 6.91 -> 6.79 µm; moving the part gives 1/sqrt(N) (0.099). The additive-noise model wrongly promises that averaging helps. Errors over aperture and triangulation angle follow the formula with slopes -0.94 / -0.99 at 0.84-0.90 of its value. Four-times-larger pixels blunt the visible contrast from 1.02 to 0.80 yet move the centroid by 1.6e-13 µm.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_laser_line_speckle_floor.py)
- [Coupe laser d'un cordon : un angle plus raide cache le caniveau dans sa propre ombre](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_weld_bead_scan_angle/02_frames.png) (figure): When a laser line traces a weld bead, a steeper camera angle measures height more finely. Even at 28 degrees, part of the undercut groove falls into its own shadow and its depth reads shallow. The shadowed span still looks measured, which makes the loss easy to miss.
  A fillet-weld cross-section is built in closed form with convexity, leg lengths, throat and undercut depth varied along the weld line by known functions, then the triangulation angle is swept from 15 to 70 degrees. Height resolution improves as 1/sin(theta) while any face rising steeper than cot(theta) hides in its own shadow, so the far parent plate turns away exactly at the predicted 37.0 degrees and the far undercut starts vanishing earlier, at 20.8-34.5 degrees (the deeper the groove, the sooner).  **The dangerous parts are that at 28 degrees every cross-section still reports a value while 25 % of the groove span is unmeasured and the depth reads 27 % shallow, and that raising the angle further drops the deepest cross-sections out of the average (true mean drifting 0.260 to 0.047 mm) — a survivorship bias; the optimal angle differs per measurand at 20 / 24 / 36 / 64 degrees.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_weld_bead_scan_angle.py)
- [Trouver et corriger les coquilles d'un panneau sans le lire](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_glyph_typo_detection/01_sign_before_after.png) (figure): Given the intended text, wrong characters in a sign image can be found without reading the text. Each cell is compared with one specified character, so no 6000-way classifier is needed. On a synthetic sign every planted error was found with no false alarms, then replaced.
  Because the intended string is given as input, no 6000-way classification is needed: each cell is compared against one specified character. The threshold is not guessed but derived from the 95th percentile of the distance between the same character in different typefaces (0.058). The distance is the 99th percentile rather than the mean — a substitution usually keeps one radical and swaps the rest, so the mean sinks the 検/横 pair to 0.0171, below the typeface floor, making it undetectable in principle.  **On a synthetic sign all four planted errors are found with no false alarms, and after replacement the distance drops from 0.0805 to 0.0325, below the floor for all four.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_glyph_typo_detection.py)
- [Évaluer le riz adventice des rizières sur photos de drone](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_weed_rice_ratio/05_real_examples.png) (figure): Aerial photos of rice paddies are graded by how much weedy rice is mixed into the crop. A linear discriminant with no neural network was scored once on 148 held-out test images. The grade matched the annotation a little over half the time, and it broke down on a date from another season.
  UAV paddy RGB is divided into 16 px blocks. A supervised ten-feature Fisher LDA is fitted on train, the grid candidate selected on validation, and 148 test images scored once. Predeclared edges are 5/25/50%.  **Agreement is 0.574 (majority 0.345), kappa 0.424, cover correlation 0.826 and IoU 0.397 (random 0.189). Swapping R/G gives kappa -0.023. Holding out flight dates, one different-season date has within-date correlation 0.14; the bundled split measures other frames from seen flights.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_weed_rice_ratio.py)
- [Déformation par corrélation d'images : enchaîner les images voisines divise l'erreur par 31](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_strain_history/01_speckle.png) (figure): A creep test is followed by photographing a speckle pattern on the part as it slowly stretches. Adding up the differences between neighbouring frames was 31x more accurate than comparing every frame with the first. What mattered was the deformation per step, not the number of comparisons.
  One hour of creep in 25 frames, with strain history from accumulating adjacent-frame displacements versus comparing each frame directly with the reference.  **At the end, cumulative errs by 61 µε and direct by 1878 µε — cumulative wins 31x and the textbook crossover in time never appears (it lives on the noise axis instead). Thinning from 24 to 4 steps worsens cumulative from -60 to -606 µε; what matters is the deformation per step, not the number of steps.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_strain_history.py)

**Mesure tridimensionnelle et traitement géométrique**

- [L'alignement masque les défauts : CAO contre scan](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_cad_scan_deviation/14_align_orbit.gif) (GIF): Aligning a scan to its design can absorb a defect or move it elsewhere. Known dents and warps were added to a part; even the defect-free control produced 66.5 square mm of false rejected area. Rigid-like deviations shrink, while nearest points jump across edges and create a fictitious dent.
  An analytic machined part carries a known local dent, a bow and a wear band, all applied as exact displacements along the surface normal, and the scan is synthesized with a known pose, noise and one-sided occlusion. After alignment the signed deviation and the out-of-tolerance area show that the local dent is diluted by only 3.7 %, while a 121 um dent appears at the centre of the part where the truth is just 1.2 um (closed form predicts -120 um).  **What survives depends on how much the defect resembles the six rigid-body degrees of freedom; along the edges the nearest neighbour jumps to the adjacent face, producing 66.5 mm^2 of false out-of-tolerance area even in a defect-free control.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_cad_scan_deviation.py)
- [Alignement image à image noté sur des données RGB-D publiques : l'erreur tombe à 0,42 fois, à peu près au niveau de l'hypothèse de vitesse constante](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_real_tum_rgbd_slam/02_trajectory.png) (figure): On public depth images of a desk, frame-to-frame alignment was checked against motion-capture truth. Error fell to 0.42 of no alignment, yet it was no better than assuming steady speed. Small-window plane residuals grew with distance, but quantisation and window size blur whether the law is a square.
  From the real RGB-D data published by the Technical University of Munich (TUM) — freiburg1_xyz, a desk scene captured with a Kinect, CC BY 4.0, Sturm, Engelhard, Endres, Burgard and Cremers, IROS 2012 — we ship 32 depth images (every 5th of 798, about 5 s, 3.9 MB) byte-for-byte as distributed (SHA-256 pinned) and score two externally obtained truths. Trajectory (motion-capture truth): `depth_to_organized_points`, `normals_from_depth` and `occlusion_edges` make points and normals; 31 neighbouring pairs are aligned with `icp_point2plane`; the relative pose error against the truth is the RPE, and the chained positions after a rigid alignment give the ATE. Three baselines. Not aligning (identity) is the weakest: its RPE is just the size of the true motion and its ATE just the spread of the path (a chain that adds the mean true motion every step also gives 155.7 mm). So a constant-velocity baseline was added (reuse the previous pair's true relative pose, an oracle). ICP's `trim` (the fraction of far correspondences dropped) had four candidates, None / 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3, picked on the first 15 pairs (0.2) and scored on the last 16: translation RPE 26.2 mm against 62.8 mm for not aligning (ratio 0.42), rotation 1.61 degrees against 3.69. That ratio is imprecise: 94 % of the second half's sum of squares comes from three pairs right after the split (pairs 16-18); without them it is 7.4 mm (median 7.9 mm), and a bootstrap over pairs gives a 95 % interval of 0.12 to 0.67 for the ratio. Against constant velocity (pairs 1-30): constant velocity 23.8 mm, ICP with trim 0.2 19.6 mm (0.82 times, about the same), trim 0.1 10.7 mm, default (no trim) 36.7 mm, not aligning 60.4 mm. ATE is 29.0 mm for ICP, 59.4 mm for the constant-velocity chain and 155.9 mm for not aligning (path 1.77 m, final drift 39.3 mm). The choice depends on the split: on the second half alone trim 0.1 is best at 13.4 mm, while the 0.2 picked on the first half gives 26.2 mm. Over eight splits (second to first, even to odd, odd to even, four folds) the ratio is 0.10 to 0.42, the split used here being the worst, and trim 0.1 is picked in seven of them. Over all 31 pairs, trim 0.1 gives 10.6 mm and 0.2 gives 19.3 mm. Replacing the ROS default intrinsics with the nominal IR values moves the all-pairs RPE from 19.3 mm to 19.7 mm. Window residuals (not a noise measurement): plane-fit residuals (`fit_plane_3d`, along the depth axis) of 6,736 windows of 24 x 24 pixels, as medians per distance bin at Z = 0.66 / 0.80 / 0.98 / 1.19 / 1.42 / 1.73 / 2.86 m, are 1.40 / 1.75 / 2.46 / 3.48 / 3.53 / 3.62 / 13.26 mm, increasing monotonically. They mix sensor noise with disparity quantisation, relief inside the window and the window's physical width (wider when farther), so they neither support nor refute the Z-squared shape of Khoshelham and Elberink 2012, Eq. (7). The log-log slope up to 2 m is 0.90 to 1.31 depending on window size and bins (1.07 for 24 pixels and the standard bins, 1.41 over all bins). Quantising a noise-free tilted plane on a disparity grid estimated from the bundled depth values (step 0.00288 per metre, alignment 0.65) gives window residuals growing with slope 1.99 and explains 59-88 % of the measured median from 1.4 m outward (30-44 % nearer). With windows of 12 / 24 / 48 pixels the median at Z = 1 m is 1.77 / 2.46 / 5.22 mm, so it is not a window-independent sensor noise. Estimator check: a tilted plane with quantisation (1/5000 m) only gives 0.052 mm, and 3 mm of added noise comes back as 2.98 mm. Large planes taken from the whole image with `plane_segmentation` plus `euclidean_cluster` leave 8.77 mm at Z = 0.9-1.1 m (53 planes), 3.6 times the 2.46 mm of windows at the same distance, because they include the desk's relief and the way planes are picked. The paper's printed coefficient |m/(f b)| = 2.85e-5 and half-pixel disparity noise are as quoted, but its unit could not be confirmed from the text, so the absolute value is marked unconfirmed and is not used in any gate. There are 21 gates (all-zero, NaN and wrong-shape depth rejected, 71-77 % valid pixels, a floor on points, the truth sampling interval, and a mark that real data was read), set after seeing the results. The window gates 'increases monotonically' and 'far side at least 1.5 times the near side' pass for Z and for Z-squared alike and cannot exclude Z-squared. Limits: no dense depth truth, so absolute depth error is not measured; one sequence of 32 frames; no head-to-head with published SLAM systems; the extrinsics between the pose truth and the depth camera are not corrected; the chosen trim changes with the split; the RPE interval is about 0.17 s (not TUM's standard 1 s). [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_real_tum_rgbd_slam.py)
- [Une pièce, cinq méthodes de mesure 3-D](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_3d_sensor_modalities/01_map.png) (figure): The same part was measured by several depth-sensing methods in matte, glossy metal, translucent and black materials. Shape errors were counted with a common 0.1 mm yardstick and compared with the dimension table. Which material breaks which method, and where, differs from method to method.
  Trade-show booths show one method on its best part. Here one part with a 3 mm step, a blind hole, a 26.6 degree slope, a V-groove, a rib and shallow dents (50 and 15 um) is measured in four materials (matte, glossy metal, translucent resin, black) by five methods (stereo, fringe projection with phase shifting, indirect ToF, light section and photometric stereo) and compared with the dimension table. Imaging is rendered physically (reflection, shadows, machining texture, subsurface scattering, lens blur and vignetting, coherent speckle, one specular bounce, V-groove interreflection, 12-bit sensor noise). Shape errors are counted on one common scale for every method, 0.1 mm and 1 mm, and a uniform offset is reported separately. Stereo, fringe projection and light section all triangulate at 30 degrees, and every method uses auto-exposure capped at 8x the matte exposure. On matte, noise is 4.5 um for fringe projection, 6.6 um for stereo, 14.3 um for light section (flat plate) and 1.34 mm for ToF. The order depends on conditions (stereo at +/-5 degrees gives 20.9 um). ToF with 0.4 mm pixels has 94% of pixels off by more than 0.1 mm even on matte. On glossy metal, 56% of the V-groove is off in fringe projection from the specular double bounce (10% on matte), and ToF, whose exposure locks onto the specular return, has 86% off by more than 1 mm. On translucent resin, stereo loses its texture (75% missing), while fringe projection sinks uniformly by -67 um but its flat-area shape errors stay at 10%. Photometric stereo loses the step by 2.9 mm (the same for three random seeds). The 50 um dent is read by photometric stereo to +7 um, fringe projection to +3 um and light section to -3 um; ToF cannot read it. Light-section speckle noise agrees with the operator's independent 2-D coherent-imaging simulation under the same conditions (ratio 0.87 to 0.96; the 13% shortfall at 0.05 mm pixels is unresolved). It is 1.09 to 1.38 times the Dorsch formula itself (10.35 um) and 0.74 to 0.85 times the value with the operator's empirical sqrt(w/L) factor (the fringe and ToF noise formulas are self-consistency checks of the same noise model). Real data support only the edge behaviour: just outside a step, correlation-based methods fatten (keep the upper height) while ToF floats between object and background. Real stereo (Middlebury 2014) fattens 39% / floats 9%; on the same T-LESS scene the structured-light depth sensor fattens 34% / floats 5% and the ToF depth sensor floats 48% / fattens 18%, and counting in the depth sensors' native pixels keeps the direction (ToF floats 52%, structured light 11%). Material parameters are the author's values; the material claims are simulation only. On the acceptance-test yardstick (the same evaluation operators as poc_virtual_acceptance_3d, two 8 mm spheres and a flat plate) the probing form error PF on matte, as the median over 200 random 200-point subsets, is 0.027 mm for fringe projection, 0.038 for photometric stereo, 0.380 for stereo and 4.87 for ToF. Light section gives 0.104 mm (5-95%: 0.077 to 0.129; 0.173 with all points), so it cannot be said to be within 0.1 mm (a limit). On glossy metal fringe projection rises to 0.238 mm. PF is a range and grows with the point count, so the density-independent residual RMS is listed too. Unlike the earlier single-method deep dives (acceptance testing, three ToF exhibits, fringe interreflection, two light-section floors, two real-stereo exhibits, photometric stereo), this one renders the same part in the same materials from raw images for all five methods and decodes them on one yardstick. The verdict 'measurable to 0.1 mm' means at most 5% of pixels beyond 0.1 mm, at most 1% beyond 1 mm and at most 20% missing. [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_3d_sensor_modalities.py)
- [Disparité, surface et pose de cercle en stéréo réelle](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_real_stereo_multiscanline/02_failure_map.png) (figure): Using a real pair of stereo images, we compute disparity, distance, surfaces and even the pose of a circle. The result is scored with the true disparity that Middlebury 2014 supplies and compared with OpenCV. Where it breaks is shown on a map.
  From a real stereo pair (Middlebury 2014 motorcycle, with ground-truth disparity), binocular_disparity_ms gives disparity, binocular_distance_ms and reconstruct_surface_stereo give distance and surface, and get_circle_pose gives the pose of the rear wheel rim; all are scored against the ground-truth disparity and against OpenCV (SGBM, reprojectImageTo3D, matchTemplate). Valid-pixel bad-2 is 4.67% with mean error 0.960 px at 90.0% density (SGBM 7.27% / 1.315 px / 88.4%). Counting invalid pixels as errors gives 14.23% (SGBM 18.00%); on pixels where both are valid it is 4.45% vs 4.95%, so half of the gap comes from which pixels each method declares invalid. On those same pixels SGBM's mean error is slightly lower (0.918 vs 0.912 px), and it runs tens to over a hundred times faster (55 to 108 times observed; the ratio swings with the measuring environment). Where it breaks: occlusions (6.9%, counted geometrically from the ground truth) are 57% invalidated by the left-right check. Errors remain near depth discontinuities (bad-2 14.1%) and on surfaces whose brightness differs between the views at the true correspondence (a proxy for reflection and gloss, 11.5%); everything else is at 0.85%. 'Textureless fails' applies only to the flattest 1.2% of pixels, but there SGBM wins clearly (bad-2 0.31% vs 4.14%, invalid 0.0% vs 14.5%); the concrete floor matches best at 0.68%. The 'ms' in the name means multi-scanline, not multi-scale: going from 2 to 4 aggregation paths takes bad-2 from 5.55% to 4.67%, and a half-resolution pass run outside the operator gives 8.31%. Distance with doffs has a median relative error of 0.37%, and the point cloud matches OpenCV to 0.0006 mm. The circle pose shows the operator is right and the geometry is ill-conditioned: on the rim (semi-major axis 62 px, 223 degrees of arc) the centre is 23.5 mm and the normal 7.74 degrees off, but points taken on the true circle give 1.2e-6 degrees. Adding noise equal to the 0.52 px gap between the edge points and the true circle gives a median normal error of 5.79 degrees (95th percentile 9.39). The two solutions differ by 0.007 px in reprojection and cannot be told apart, yet their normals are 50.7 degrees apart. The same edge lifted to 3-D with the estimated disparity gives 2.3 mm and 1.59 degrees. points_lepetit with crop_rectangle2, create_ncc_model and find_ncc_models gives bad-2 13.8% (random pixels 16.3%), 2.2% above score 0.9, with best positions identical to OpenCV's matchTemplate at every point. On the 15 MiddEval3 training scenes (non-occluded) the median bad-2 is 6.52% vs SGBM 10.20%, lower in 14 scenes on valid pixels but in 12 when invalid pixels count as errors (SGBM is better on Playroom, Playtable and Shelves). The SGBM reference uses near-default settings that were not tuned. Changing exposure or lighting barely moves it (-0.18 / +0.94 points vs SGBM +4.99 / +18.08; under lighting it invalidates more pixels). But on the pair that differs only in rectification (Playtable, mean vertical residual 0.24 px) it worsens by 12.1 points while SGBM moves 0.6: it is sensitive to vertical misalignment (cause not yet verified). [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_real_stereo_multiscanline.py)
- [Mire à pastilles de bout en bout : fx dévie de 0,45 % même sans bruit, et le barycentre binaire fixe ce plancher](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_caltab_chain_truth/01_caltab_chain.png) (figure): A calibration plate with circular marks is imaged and taken from point detection to camera calibration, then scored against the true camera. Even without noise the focal length is off by 0.45 %. That floor is set by the centroid of the binarised marks.
  A create_caltab circle-mark plate (7x9, 15 mm pitch) is imaged by a camera with a true K, barrel distortion (k1 = −0.18) and eight poses. The image is rendered by tracing each pixel back to the plate (3x3 samples, 0.7 px blur), so it shares no formula with the estimator. find_caltab finds all 63 marks and camera_calibration_lm calibrates from them. Without noise the detected marks are 0.0939 px off, matching the quantisation of a binary centroid, 1/sqrt(6πρ) = 0.0934 px for ρ = 6.08 px. At image noise σ_I = 0.05 all eight intrinsic and distortion errors are within 2 σ of the LM covariance (whitened distance d² = 11.2 against the χ²₈ 99.9 % point of 26.1). fx is off by −0.42 %, and by −0.45 % even without noise, so most of the error is the quantisation floor. Pose errors are 0.23° and 2.4 mm in depth, at most half the tolerance propagated from the intrinsic 1 σ. Sweeping the noise from 0.02 to 0.2, the fx deviation grows in proportion to the detection deviation (log-log slope 0.99) at 0.85-0.92 of the gain predicted by the covariance. The detection deviation itself grows only as the 0.56 power of the image noise, so halving the image noise shrinks the calibration error by just 1/√2. k2 has a 1 σ of 0.051, as large as its true value of 0.06, and came out with the wrong sign. [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_caltab_chain_truth.py)
- [LiDAR aéroporté à deux dates : sans retirer le décalage, tout semble creusé](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_lidar_terrain_change/13_real_dod.png) (figure): Subtracting airborne point clouds of a site taken in different years shows how much ground was dug away. When the changed area covers 33 % of the scene, the alignment absorbs the change itself and the earthwork volume collapses. On public real data, the whole area looks excavated until a systematic offset is removed.
  A synthetic slope carries an excavation and a deposit of known volume; two epochs of airborne points are differenced both vertically (DoD) and along the local surface normal (M3C2). The expected "cos-θ shrinkage on a slope" never appears — integrating vertical differences over horizontal area cancels the cosine, and the excavated volume stays within -0.011 % from 0 to 40 degrees.  **What breaks is the registration: when the changed area covers 33 % of the scene, ICP absorbs the change itself and the net volume collapses from a true -30.4 m3 to -3.8 m3, while a no-change control alone already fabricates 83.8 m3 of phantom excavation.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_lidar_terrain_change.py)
- [Stéréo réelle : une plage de disparité trop courte perd tout le premier plan](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_real_stereo_depth/01_scene.png) (figure): A public real stereo photograph with true disparity is used to check depth measurement. Setting the disparity search below the true maximum loses the whole foreground, with 95.06 % of pixels wrong. When converting to distance, ignoring a single calibration value bends the shape.
  The first exhibit in this museum measured on a **real photograph** (Middlebury 2014 motorcycle, with ground-truth disparity). The distributor's own note says the holes in the truth are NaN; they are +inf, so np.nanmedian reports a median disparity of 44.97 px instead of 42.55 px, while fill_disparity and apply_cmap — which test with isfinite — handle them correctly.  **The default max_disp=16 scores bad2 95.06 %: any search range below the true maximum of 59.91 px loses the whole foreground, so the cliff sits between 48 and 64, which geometry predicts before any measurement. Against a 94.04 % null, SGM scores 15.81 %, and discarding the least confident 40 % takes it to 6.80 %. Converting to metric depth bends the scene: depth_from_disparity had no principal-point offset, and ignoring the real doffs of 31.086 px puts points 1.519 to 5.243 times too far — no single scale repairs it, since the best factor of 0.3733 still leaves 958.3 mm RMS over a 2889 mm range, pushing far surfaces +1676 mm out and pulling near ones -926 mm in. The argument was added here, and it reproduces the closed form exactly.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_real_stereo_depth.py)
- [Volume d'un stock : le sol et l'arrière invisibles fixent l'erreur](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_stockpile_volume/07_scan_orbit.mp4) (vidéo): Stockpile inventory at mines and ports comes from scanning the pile and computing its volume. Filling the hidden back side by interpolation overstated the volume by +17.20 %. The answer hinges on an assumption about the ground beneath the pile, which nobody measures.
  En tournant autour du tas, le nombre de positions de scan 3-D passe de 1 à 3. La couleur représente la différence entre la surface interpolée et la surface réelle. **L'erreur sur la quantité en stock passe de +17.20 % à +0.05 % (base réelle ; la vérité terrain du volume est la forme fermée 3572.6089 m³).** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_stockpile_volume.py)

**Tomographie à rayons X et traitement de volumes**

- [Pores sur radiographie de soudure : un seuil fixe prend aussi le bord du cordon](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_weld_radiograph_porosity/02_map_detections.png) (figure): Miscounting pores in a weld radiograph leads to the wrong weld grade. A fixed cut-off also counts the edge of the weld cap and reports 124 blobs. Estimating the background before detection keeps only real pores, and far fewer images are graded wrongly.
  A radiograph drawn in closed form with Beer–Lambert (exponential absorption law): 10 mm plate, arc-shaped reinforcement, spherical pores, plus scatter, unsharpness and film grain. The fixed-threshold baseline counts the weld toe as pores (124 blobs, total area 15.19 mm² against 7.93 true). The detection cliff is predictable from CNR = 16.12·d²: Rose's CNR = 4 misses (0.50 mm), while the prediction that includes smoothing and the 3 px minimum area gives 0.58 mm against 0.57 mm measured. The 9 px window cap of the background-estimation op (rectangular opening) becomes a cliff: detection drops below 50 % from 2.0 mm and reaches 0 % at 2.5 mm — choosing the op chooses the measuring range. Scatter at SPR = 1 shrinks the volumetric diameter by (1+SPR)^(-1/3): -22.6 % measured against -20.6 % predicted.  **Images misgraded by one class: 90 % for the baseline, 23 % with the volumetric diameter.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_weld_radiograph_porosity.py)
  Terms: Beer–Lambert — The law stating that transmission through a homogeneous absorbing medium decreases exponentially with concentration and optical path length.
- [Rugosité des faces orientées vers le bas sur un vrai scanner d'une pièce de fabrication additive](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_am_thermal_to_ct/07_ct_hole_crown.png) (figure): Parts made by metal additive manufacturing are examined in public X-ray CT slices. Down-facing surfaces showed a protrusion of 63 micrometres, about twice the up-facing ones. At comparable slopes the gap shrinks, and no link to the thermal images is claimed yet.
  Thermography is a single track on bare In718; CT is Overhang Part X4 from a separate experiment and build, so no linkage is claimed. Recorded values: 4095 DL is 1401 C at emissivity 1 and 1641 C at 0.3.  **CT/design Dice is 0.981; protrusion is 63 micrometres down-facing versus 32 up-facing, but only 1.16 times at comparable slope. No internal void was found above about 63 micrometres.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_am_thermal_to_ct.py)

**Optique, interférométrie et polarisation**

- [Un creux invisible sur photo apparaît dans le reflet des franges](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_phase_measuring_deflectometry/03_photo_vs_pmd.png) (figure): A very shallow dent in glossy paint is practically invisible in an ordinary photograph. Projecting fringes on a screen in front of the surface and reading how their mirror image bends gives curvature, and a dent 3 µm deep stands out clearly. The flat-looking photo sits next to curvature maps in which the dents appear.
  A dent a few micrometres deep in a glossy surface (a painted car panel, a cover glass) is all but invisible in a lit photograph, because a mirror only shows the light. Put a screen in front of the surface, show phase-shifted fringes and photograph their mirror image: a tilt of 1e-3 rad moves the reflected point 0.6 mm on a screen 30 cm away. Six new ops (`deflectometry`, category `deflectometry` of `opsspecular`): the rig, the screen pattern, the forward render (height field to fringe mirror image, with a diffuse fraction, scatter, the back reflection of glass, aperture-integrated focus and noise), phase to normals (via `match3d.normal_from_reflection`), slope to curvature (one derivative, no integration) and the closed-form sensitivity.  **On a painted panel with 3 µm and 1 µm dents, 0.8 µm waviness and 0.12 µm polishing marks the photograph's defect signal peaks at 0.00066 (0.17 of one 8-bit step) while the curvature map shows the dents at SNR 110 and 57, and the integrated depth is within 0.5 % of the truth. Phase noise matches sqrt(2/N) sigma/b at 1.00 and slope noise is 0.67 of sigma_u/(2d), the sqrt(4/9) of bilinear resampling. Where it breaks: lifting the part by 0.3 mm adds the apparent tilt dz sin(theta)/d (to 0.1 %) and a 388 m bowl while the dent depth moves 0.7 %; a 2 % screen-distance error makes dents 2.3 % shallower; a 1 µm dent survives a diffuse fraction of 0.85 and fringe orders start to jump at 0.9; on uncoated cover glass the back-surface light is bent the other way by the dent's wedge, so the slope sensitivity shrinks to the closed-form 0.246 (measured 0.246) and dents look a quarter as deep; with an 8 mm aperture, focusing on the surface leaves 0.60 of the fringe amplitude and focusing on the screen leaves 0.52 of the depth. Nothing learned, nothing calibrated, 22 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_phase_measuring_deflectometry.py)
- [Épaisseur des couches de peinture sans découpe (OCT industrielle)](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_industrial_oct/01_real_paint_bscans.png) (figure): We want to see the layers of paint and multilayer film in depth without cutting them. Clear-coat thicknesses computed from public raw interference spectra of automotive paint agree with the paper's OCT values within 1.5 µm. Layers too thin to separate are reported as unmeasurable instead of guessed.
  Optical coherence tomography sees the layer stack of paints, conformal coatings, adhesive layers and multilayer films, and voids and delaminations inside them, without cutting. Thirteen new ops (`octscan`, category `oct` of `opsoptics`): closed-form design numbers before measuring, interferogram simulators driven by the material table, k-linearisation, dispersion compensation and estimation, A- and B-scans, layer thickness through the group index (refusing layers that cannot be separated), boundary tracing, surface flattening and an entry point for raw spectra.  **Real data: public raw spectra of automotive paint panels (CC BY 4.0) processed with Fullseye ops alone give clear-coat thicknesses within 1.5 µm of the paper's OCT and 3.6 µm of the microscope, base coats within 2.3 µm of the microscope for three of four panels (one is 7.6 µm off and unresolved), a refusal under the white base coat, and an autocorrelation ghost lying flat at the clear coat's optical thickness. Synthetic: thickness through the group index within 0.04 µm of the truth, SNR, roll-off and dispersion broadening matching the closed forms, refusal below 1.4 point-spread widths, a coating-thickness map and void/delamination detection.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_industrial_oct.py)
  Terms: OCT — A non-contact method that uses low-coherence interference to measure depth-resolved reflectivity and form cross-sectional images.
- [Trouver des corps étrangers couleur viande par imagerie spectrale](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_hyperspectral_foreign_matter/04_real_pork_belly.png) (figure): Foreign matter the same colour as meat or kernels cannot be told apart by a colour camera. Classifying public real images as spectral data detected debris on pork belly with 1.1 % false alarms. With fewer bands the false alarms grew, so a band set that sufficed on synthetic data did not suffice on real data.
  Two public hyperspectral datasets (both CC BY 4.0; fetched and cached, not committed) are classified with Fullseye's ops (read_envi, spec_continuum_removal, spec_angle_mapper, spec_band_selection_fisher) and scored against their own labels. Shredded plastics, six classes (RODARE 3390, a 400–2500 nm spectral camera): continuum removal + SAM reaches a mean class recall of 0.816 (0.439 on raw reflectance — dark flakes lose to additive baselines). A library from a separate scan of reference samples separates PP / PET / PE at 0.878; this PoC's material-table physics alone gives 0.628 (chance 0.333). Black plastic reflects 0.03 with a C-H trough one sixth as deep. The test header still carried 450 fwhm values for 360 bands and the fail-closed reader refused it; the wavelength labels were verified on the polymer troughs and only fwhm was fixed in a copy.  **Foreign objects on pork belly, ten classes (Zenodo 17242553, an InGaAs line-scan spectral camera): split by capture, 656k test pixels; SAM gives AUC 0.92 with 10 % false alarms, LDA on all 184 bands detects 0.92 at 1.1 % false alarms, six Fisher bands give 2.1 % and three give 23 % — three bands sufficed in simulation but not on real data. The simulation (material absorption bands, Kubelka–Munk, halogen × fused-silica lens × InGaAs; eight new ops) covers the failure modes: colour-matched foreign matter at RGB AUC 0.48, kernels at 12 % moisture 98 % rejected, a 200 K lamp drift after the white reference 49 %, and carbon black at 1 wt % burying PP vs PE in single-pixel noise. The gap between simulated 1.000 and real 0.82–0.92 is the size of the inverse crime. Parameters were checked against primary sources (USGS spectra, papers, datasheets): PE band strengths cut 2–3x, PET's two deepest bands added, and the table's water k switched to Segelstein 1981.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_hyperspectral_foreign_matter.py)
  Terms: SAM — A classifier that compares spectra by the angle between their vectors, making it invariant to a common multiplicative scale.
- [Épaisseur d'oxyde cartographiée d'après les couleurs d'une micrographie](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_thin_film_reflectometry/01_real_micrograph_thickness.png) (figure): Oxide thickness on silicon can be read from interference fringes and colours in reflected light. From a real micrograph published by other researchers, a per-pixel thickness map was drawn, centred on 87.4 nm. It stays below the reference value, and the camera's colour sensitivity is unknown.
  Five ops (`filmoptics`, category `film` of `opsoptics`) measure the thickness of SiO₂ on silicon from the interference fringes of its reflectance; films and substrates are keys of the material table. The core is scoring on other people's measured data (both CC BY 4.0). In the microspectroscopy of Schwarz et al. (2024) the measured reflectance of bare silicon agrees with the table's silicon to 0.4 % (median); a normal-incidence fit gives 92.9 nm against the 98.5 nm reference (−5.7 %), and choosing the NA by the residual (0.5) gives 95.8 nm (−2.7 %); the coverage of the measurement spot is read back over 11 spectra with a mean error of 0.6 points. From the real colour micrograph of the same sample, a white balance on the bare silicon alone gives a per-pixel thickness map with a median of 87.4 nm (−11 %, camera sensitivities unknown). With the low-cost spectrometers of Sánchez-Arriaga et al. (2023) the 476.3 nm standard is within 2 % in all five spectra; the 198.7 nm standard reads a median of 6.6 % thick, and in one spectrum the best candidate is 51 nm while the right value is the second candidate. The FFT method flags the 98 nm film as unreliable (closed-form limit 554 nm).  **Synthetic chapters: Airy agreement 3e-15, fringe count within one, FFT limit 538 nm closed form versus 525 nm measured, closed-form biases for wrong index, angle and NA, 59 % hue agreement with the published colour chart in the first three orders (a weak gate), and a modelled wafer (radial comet to the edge, edge bead, off-centre rings, each tied to the literature or labelled a model) mapped within 0.17 nm RMS by a 31-band camera while 14 % of RGB pixels keep several candidates. Nothing learned, 37 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_thin_film_reflectometry.py)
- [MEB virtuel : la tension change l'image et la largeur de ligne](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_virtual_sem_kv_cd_bse/05_sem_same_part_3kv_3detectors.png) (figure): An SEM image is not a photo of the surface but a tally of electrons escaping after scattering inside the solid. A model that tracks each electron was checked against measured backscatter data for 7 elements. On the same line, the beam energy decides which sidewall height is read as the linewidth.
  An SEM image is not a photograph of the surface: it is a tally of what comes out of the interaction volume of primary electrons scattering inside the solid. Eight new ops (`sem_electron_mc`, `sem_yield_curve`, `sem_se_universal_curve`, `sem_line_profile`, `sem_cd_measure`, `sem_image`, `sem_charging_series`, `sem_se_escape_fraction`) and the material-table op `material_electron_properties` build a Monte Carlo that tracks electrons one by one (Joy's single scattering, Browning's 1994 Mott fit for elastic scattering, the Joy-Luo stopping power), and every stage is scored against outside measurements. The backscatter coefficient for 7 elements (C to Au) at 5/10/20/30 keV falls inside the measured bands of Joy's database plus or minus 0.04; switching back to screened Rutherford fails for W and Au. The maximum penetration is 0.83 to 1.04 of the Kanaya-Okayama range and the depth grows as E^1.75 (Si) and E^1.52 (Au) against 1.67. The shape of the secondary-electron yield matches the Lin and Joy 2005 universal curve within RMS 0.076 for C to Ag, with Emax within -4 to +7 % of their table (not fitted); W and Au have heavier tails (SE2 dominates, unresolved). The user's guess that W on Si is invisible in SE but stands out in backscatter Z contrast holds only for flush W at 1 kV (SE +5 %, BSE +96 %); at 5 kV SE shows it too (+80 %) because the SE2 made by exiting backscattered electrons carries Z. W buried 150 nm deep shows +3 % at 1 kV even in backscatter and +154 % at 20 kV (information depth). For CD-SEM, a PMMA line (40 nm at the base) with an 88 degree sidewall measures 39.1 nm at 1 kV and 39.9 nm at 20 kV by threshold: low voltage measures high on the sidewall (0.33), high voltage at the foot (0.03); at 80 degrees the difference is small and reversed, so this is no general rule.  **A vertical sidewall reads wide at every voltage (+0.3 to +1.0 nm). Charging: at 0.5 kV (eta + delta > 1) the insulator charges positive, darkens and stops at the potential that solves eta + delta F(V*) = 1 by an independent bisection (to 2e-15 V); at 20 kV it keeps charging negative and brightens (phenomenological). A defect found on the way is kept on record: free paths were drawn in the starting material and walked straight through material boundaries, so a 0.01 nm Si cap changed W's eta from 0.425 to 0.329 (now truncated at the boundary, with a regression gate). No real-image chapter yet: no CC BY CD-SEM images with ground-truth line widths were found.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_virtual_sem_kv_cd_bse.py)
- [Les contraintes d'un disque comprimé en franges (photoélasticité)](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_photoelasticity/03_load_and_isoclinics.mp4) (vidéo): Viewed through polarisers, a loaded transparent plastic shows its internal stress as fringes. Stress in a squeezed disc was recovered from polarisation images, including pixels where the fringe order exceeds 0.5 and the phase wraps. Most errors left after unwrapping came from the sign ambiguity, not the wrapping.
  Tourner l’analyseur déplace les franges photoélastiques et modifie l’intensité moyenne dans le disque (sur 0–135°) **L'ordre de frange au centre est de 2.38 (conforme à la forme fermée). Le système polarimétrique de l'op s'écarte au plus de 2.2e-16 de la formule du manuel.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_photoelasticity.py)
  Terms: Mueller matrix — A real 4-by-4 matrix acting on a Stokes vector to describe how an optical element or sample changes polarization.
- [Empiler les étages TDI fait ressortir les défauts faibles sur une ligne sombre](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_tdi_linescan/01_tdi_stages_defects.png) (figure): Moving material such as film or steel strip is imaged with a line sensor. On a dark line, stacking 64 stages of exposure lifts faint scratches and inclusions out of the texture. If transport and capture drift out of sync, the stacked image smears.
  Moving material (film, steel strip, wafers) is imaged with line sensors, and on a dark line TDI (time-delay integration) adds N stages of exposure. Six new ops (`linescan`, category `linescan` of `opsoptics`): TDI SNR from the EMVA 1288 linear model (charge-domain and digital summation), the closed-form MTF of a synchronisation error and of yaw, an acquisition that transports a reflectance map past the sensor (speed error, yaw, weave, encoder sync, lens, noise), a ray-traced profile of illumination and MTF across the image height, per-column lens maps and two example lenses. On a dark cold-rolled steel strip at 40 photons per stage, the SNR measured from the temporal noise of image pairs matches equation 21 within 1.2 % at every stage count. Digital summation gains exactly √N (×8.09 at 64 stages), charge summation pays the read noise once and gains ×26.9. Detection of a faint scratch, pinholes and an inclusion goes from 0 of 3 at one stage to 3 of 3 at 64 stages with no false alarms. A speed error ε smears the image by N·ε pixels and the scan MTF follows |sinc(f N ε)|, printed first and then measured on a sine chart: largest difference 0.0035 over 45 conditions. A ±3 % speed ripple drops the local MTF to 0.64 when free-running and is fully removed by encoder sync; the cross-direction blur of yaw, |sinc(f N sin θ)|, is not (largest difference 0.0017). The same triplet optimised for a 57 mm line (f 90) and for a 2/3-inch format (f 35, a C-mount lens reused) was ray-traced: illumination splits exactly into cos⁴ × pupil × distortion (2e-16), the line-scan lens keeps 0.976 at the end of the sensor (cos⁴ alone gives 0.855; barrel distortion of −2.9 % adds ×1.111), and the C-mount lens delivers no light beyond 19.7 mm.  **Flat-field correction evens the brightness (0.961 to 1.000) but leaves the sine modulation unchanged (difference 0.0006), and the CNR of an inclusion at 14 mm stays at 1.52 to 1.54 with the C-mount lens against 2.49 with the line-scan lens. Nothing learned, nothing calibrated, 19 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_tdi_linescan.py)
  Terms: TDI — A line-sensor technique that sums charge or signals over multiple stages in step with image motion to increase exposure and SNR.; EMVA 1288 — A standard measurement procedure and set of quantities for sensitivity, noise, saturation, and non-uniformity of machine-vision image sensors.; MTF — The magnitude of the optical transfer function, showing how image contrast is transferred at each spatial frequency.
- [Où la profondeur ToF indirecte se brise](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_itof_multifreq/12_public_raw_unwrap.png) (figure): An indirect ToF camera measures distance from the phase delay of modulated light. Combining three frequencies keeps the distance unambiguous out to 18.7 m. Multipath and object edges make the distance jump without any warning.
  Continuous-wave indirect time of flight (modulated light, correlation sampled at four phase steps) taken from raw buckets to depth and into its failure modes. Eight new ops (`itof`, category `itof` of `opsoptics`): a capture model with shot noise, ambient light, extra paths and pixel-aperture mixing; phase, amplitude and offset; the closed-form noise budget; multi-frequency unwrapping by exhaustive maximum-likelihood hypotheses, `residue_crt` or the two-frequency beat; a wrong-order probability bound; two-path separation by matrix pencil; a flying-pixel mask; and cyclic-error correction. Depth noise matches c/(4πf)·√((B+σ_r²)/2)/A to 0.999-1.029 over 11 conditions with SNR ≥ 3, and Lange's formula exactly. 16/80/120 MHz is unambiguous to 18.7 m; the wrong-order rate is 0.116 at σ_φ = 0.33 rad (union bound 0.132). On the same raw signal `residue_crt` gives 0.1161, on par with maximum likelihood, because it now picks its anchor the Wang-Xia way (the band with the smallest noise variance); anchoring on 16 MHz, as before the fix, gave 0.556. With 80/100/120 MHz, shelves beyond 7.5 m silently fold to near range. A box under packaging film is pulled forward at one frequency (matching `tof_multipath_phase`); an 8-frequency matrix pencil (20-160 MHz) recovers every pixel to 2 cm at 0.8 m separation but nothing at 0.2 m or less. A room corner reads 8-99 mm too far everywhere, matching the one-bounce transport to 1e-15 m. The mask flags 97 % of the pixels floating off box edges and removes 0.03 % of step-free pixels. The ±84.8 mm square-wave cyclic error matches atan(u/(1−u)) and inverts to the 2.1 mm noise floor. Sunlight at 50 times the signal raises far wrong-order errors from 12 % to 93 %. Direct ToF on the same tabletop neither floats edges nor shifts under the film.  **On public raw sensor data (3 frequencies × 3 steps; Lawin, Forssén, Ovrén, ECCV 2016; Zenodo; CC BY 4.0) jumps over 30 cm are 0.131 for maximum likelihood and 0.131 for `residue_crt` (10-frame average; 0.293 before the anchor fix); discarding low-margin pixels keeps 68 % at 0.020. Nothing learned; calibrated only by 16 rail points and the left half of the real data; 30 gates.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_itof_multifreq.py)
- [Mêmes particules, tailles différentes de quelques nm entre MEB et MET](../../articles/assets/poc/poc_sem_tem_same_particle/08_tem_focus_sweep.gif) (GIF): The same nanoparticles are sized in both a scanning and a transmission electron image. Even for a 52 nm particle, the reading moved by nanometres with the imaging mode and cut-off level. Scored against a shared truth, both biases follow simple formulas for edge blur and defocus.
  One ledger of fourteen spherical and faceted particles drives both images. Area-equivalent diameter, maximum slope, watershed splitting of touching pairs, stereo height and limited-angle reconstruction are scored against the shared truth.  **For a 52 nm particle the scanning-image threshold is +4.16 nm, the transmission image is -0.18 nm at focus and +9.82 nm at 12000 nm defocus. The scanning bias follows the closed-form Gaussian rim width 2 sqrt(2 ln 2) sigma, while the dark transmission fringe follows sqrt(lambda defocus) within ten per cent. Material contrast is checked against published backscatter coefficients; 3-D measurements are checked against parallel-projection geometry and a full-angle reconstruction. No new operators, no learning and no calibration.** [source](https://github.com/furuse-kazufumi/fullseye/blob/master/examples/poc_sem_tem_same_particle.py)
